<!-- page 164 -->
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT
SERVICES
STAFF REPORT
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Hearing Date: August 26 th, 2025
Case No: TXT-A 25-002
Action: Zoning Ordinance Text Amendment
Project Name: Navajo County Assisted Living Homes Administrative
Procedures, Guest Houses/Accessory Dwelling Units, and
Definitions Update
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Request Description : Consideration of an update to Section 1916 (assisted living home
administrative procedures), Sections 502, 602, 702, and 1917 (guest houses/accessory
dwelling units), and Article 23 (definitions) of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Text Amendment, as shown in the
attached documents.
BACKGROUND/ANALYSIS:
Section 1916 – Assisted Living Homes Administrative Procedures
Already signed into law is A.R.S. § 11-820.02.  This law requires the following:
 If a county has a zoning regulation that restricts the distance between assisted living
homes, the county shall establish an administrative procedure in which a deviation
from the separation requirement may be granted as a reasonable accommodation under
the fair housing act.
 On registration as an assisted living home or when a land use application for an assisted
living home is filed, the county shall provide written notice of the administrative
procedure to request a reasonable accommodation as prescribed in above.
 If a county has a zoning regulation that restricts the distance between assisted living
homes, the distance shall be measured on a linear foot basis from lot line to lot line.
 For the purposes of the law, “assisted living home” is defined as, “a residential care
institution, including an adult foster care home, that provides or contracts to provide

<!-- page 165 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e2 | 
supervisory care services, personal care services or directed care services on a
continuous basis and provides resident rooms to ten or fewer residents.”
Navajo County already has provisions in place that measure distance between group homes
linearly based on the closest distance between property lines.  Due to this, the only change
necessary for the Ordinance is adding an administrative procedure for distance reduction as a
reasonable accommodation.
In order to bring compliance with State law, Planning and Development Services recommend
the following:
 Add a subparagraph named “Reasonable Accommodations” under "Performance
Standards” for Group Homes for the Disabled, which allows applicants to
administratively have a deviation from Navajo County’s twelve-hundred foot (1200’)
separation required between these types of group homes.
Sections 502, 602, 702, and 1917 – Guest Houses/Accessory Dwelling Units
During the 2025 Arizona Legislative Session, House Bill 2928 was introduced, passed, and
signed by the Governor into law.  This new law will create A.R.S. § 11-810.01 and is set to take
effect on September 26th, 2025.  This law has been passed regarding accessory dwelling units
(ADUs) and requirements for Counties regarding regulation of these dwellings.  As a summary,
the legislation requires the following:
 Counties shall adopt regulations that allow on any lot or parcel where single-family
dwellings are allowed:
o At least one attached and one detached ADU as a permitted use.
o A minimum of one additional detached ADU as a permitted use on a lot or parcel
that is one acre or more in size.
Within the law, there are also provisions for what counties may not do regarding accessory
dwelling units, including:
 Prohibit the use or advertisement of single-family dwellings or any ADUs as separately
leased, long-term rental housing.
 Require a familial, marital, employment, or other preexisting relationship between the
owner/occupant of a single-family dwelling and the occupant of an ADU located on the
same lot or parcel.
 Require that a lot or parcel have additional parking to accommodate an ADU or require
payment of fees instead of additional parking.
 Require that an ADU match the exterior design, roof pitch, or finishing materials of the
single-family dwelling that is located on the same lot as the accessory dwelling unit.
 Set restrictions for ADUs that are more restrictive than those for single-family dwellings
within the same zoning area with regard to height, setbacks, lot size, coverage, or
building frontage.
 Set rear or side setbacks for ADU units that are more than five feet (5’) from the property
line.
 Require improvements to public streets as a condition of allowing an ADU, except as
necessary to reconstruct or repair a public street that is disturbed as a result of the

<!-- page 166 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e3 | 
construction of the ADU.
 Require a restrictive covenant concerning an ADU on a lot or parcel zoned for
residential use by a single-family dwelling.
Of importance with the new law is that if a county fails to adopt development regulations as
required by A.R.S. § 11-810.01 by January 1st, 2026, ADUs shall be allowed on all lots or parcels
zoned for residential use in the county without limits.
The recommended updates that Planning and Development Services Staff have for guest
houses are as follows:
 Add provisions for guest houses to be permitted according to law on lots under one (1)
acre in size, as well as lots one (1) acre or more in size.
 Remove allowed zoning districts for guest houses that conflict with required provisions
of the new law.
 Remove minimum lot size requirements that conflict with required provisions of the
new law.
 Remove size limitation requirements from guest house provisions.
 Add provisions for guest houses to be allowed within five feet (5’) of rear and side lots
lines, in order to conform with the new legislation.
 Remove additionally required information that does not prove useful in reviewing guest
house permit applications.
 Clarify that guest house permits are not required for attached guest houses.
Article 23 – Definitions
In updating the Zoning Ordinance, it has become necessary to add some new definitions or
revise existing definitions.  The recommended definition additions and changes are as follows:
 Better define “Guest House” to be the same as introduced into State law.
 Introduce “Accessory Dwelling Unit” to be defined the same as Guest House, to prevent
any confusion with the new State law regarding ADUs.
 Amend the definitions of “Manufactured Home Park” and “Recreational Vehicle Park”
to not include guest houses, as this would trigger additional permitting not allowable by
State law otherwise.
 Introduce definitions for properties that qualify as non-residential and residential for
wireless communication facilities, which was left out of the Zoning Ordinance.
As of August 14 th, 2025, zero (0) comments have been received regarding this update to the
Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.
PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the proposed
amendments to the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.
Attachments:

<!-- page 167 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e4 | 
1. Track changes version of the amendments.
2. Clean version with all changes accepted for the amendments.

<!-- page 168 -->
The following Sections and Articles of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance are hereby
proposed to be amended as follows (new text is shown in underline, and text proposed to
be deleted is shown as strikeout):
Section 502 – Use Regulations
The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Single-Family
Residential Zoning Districts:
1. Guest houses.
2. Home occupations.
Section 602 – Use Regulations
The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Multi-Family Zoning
Districts:
1. Guest houses.
2. Home occupations.
Section 702 – Use Regulations
The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Commercial-Residential
Zoning District:
1. Guest houses.
Section 1916 – Group Homes
Group Homes for the Disabled
1. Purpose: The purpose of these regulations is to permit disabled persons, as defined
by state and federal law, to reside in single-family residences in compliance with
the Fair Housing Act and Arizona state law, while maintaining the residential
character of neighborhoods.
2. Applicability:
a. Zoning Confirmation: Prior to registration, requests for zoning
confirmation may be submitted to the Department to confirm the proposed
location of the group home is permitted per this Section.
b. Group Homes for the Disabled Serving Six (6) or Fewer Residents: Group
Homes for the Disabled that treat six (6) or fewer persons are not governed
by this Section and shall only be subject to the regulations of the Zoning

<!-- page 169 -->
District in which the Group Home is established, in the same manner as any
other single-family residence, per A.R.S. § 36-582.
c. Additional Requirements of State Law: Notwithstanding the foregoing, if
the State has adopted laws or rules for the regulation of a specific type of
home, such as a Group Home for the Developmentally Disabled or an
Assisted Living Home as defined by state statutes, then any such State law
or rule shall apply in addition to the conditions listed herein and shall
preempt any conflicting condition listed herein.
3. Performance Standards: Group Homes for the Disabled shall be located,
developed, and operated in compliance with the following standards:
a. Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1,200
feet as prescribed by A.R.S. § 36-582, as measured by the closest property
lines.
b. Occupancy: The number of residents, excluding staff, shall not exceed 10.
c. Exterior Appearance: There shall be no sign or other exterior indication of
a group home visible from a street.
d. Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety
Regulations: Group homes shall be required to comply with all relevant
building, environmental health, and fire safety regulations. If a group home
has one or more non-ambulatory residents, building code requirements in
addition to those applicable to group homes containing no non-ambulatory
residents, shall apply.
e. Licensing: Group homes shall comply with applicable licensing
requirements.
f. Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on-site.
g. Reasonable Accommodations: Group homes proposing to be located within
the 1,200-foot separation may be granted reasonable accommodations to
locate where proposed when it is found that the applicant has demonstrated
by a preponderance of evidence that the proposed group home will not
negatively affect nearby residents without disabilities, or the ability of
nearby group homes to help their residents integrate into the community.
Nor will the proposed group home, in combination with any existing group

<!-- page 170 -->
home, alter the residential character of the surrounding neighborhood by
creating an institutional atmosphere or de facto social service district by
clustering group homes in a neighborhood.
4. Permits and Administration:
a. Property is considered a Group Home for the Disabled under this Ordinance
after the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Department issues a Permit
for a Group Home for the Disabled for that property. Only properties
serving occupants who fit the definition of disabled under the Fair Housing
Act are eligible for determination as a Group Home for the Disabled.
b. In order to secure a permit for a group home for the disabled, an application
must be submitted on the form prescribed by the Department. The
application must include the following:
i. A site plan.
ii. A notarized statement, detailing qualifications.
iii. Copies of applicable licenses.
iv. Affidavit of compliance and other documentation indicating the
property as a group home for the disabled.
v. Any additional information, or plans, if deemed necessary.
c. If standards are not met, the home shall be considered an institutional
residence or other group home and shall be subject to the provisions of the
Zoning Ordinance regulating such establishments.  Status as a Group Home
for the Disabled is open to review by the Department and may be cancelled
at any time, upon a determination by the Director that the property is no
longer being used for a qualifying purpose or meeting standards laid forth
in this Ordinance.
Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes
1. Purpose: The purpose of these regulations is to provide guidelines for permitting
group homes which do not qualify as group homes for the disabled.
2. Applicability:

<!-- page 171 -->
a. Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the State has adopted laws or rules for the
regulation of a specific type of group home as defined by State statutes, then
any such State law or rule shall apply in addition to the conditions listed
herein and shall preempt any conflicting condition listed herein.
3. Performance Standards: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall be
located, developed, and operated in compliance with the following standards:
a. Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1200
feet as measured by the closest property lines. These group homes shall also
not be located within 1200 feet from any religious, educational, or childcare
facilities.
b. Zoning: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall only be
allowed in the R-2, R-3, and C-R zoning districts upon acquiring a Special
Use Permit.
c. Occupancy: Occupancy shall be regulated within the zoning districts as
listed below:
i. R-2 and R-3: One resident for every bedroom within the residence.
ii. C-R: Up to two residents for every bedroom within the facility.
d. Exterior Appearance: Within R-2 and R-3 zoning districts, there shall be no
sign or exterior indication of a group home visible from the street. Within C-
R zoning, signage and exterior shall comply with the regulations of the
zoning district. For group homes within the R-2 and R-3 zoning districts, a
solid wall or fence acceptable to the Director of Planning and Development
Services shall be required around any side and rear yards.
e. Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety
Regulations: Group homes shall be required to comply with all relevant
building, environmental health, and fire safety regulations. If a group home
has one or more non-ambulatory residents, building code requirements in
addition to those applicable to group homes containing no non-ambulatory
residents, shall apply.
f. Licensing: Group Homes shall comply with applicable licensing
requirements.

<!-- page 172 -->
g. Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on site and comply with
all parking standards. No parking will be allowed on any street or within any
right-of-way.
4. Permits and Administration:
a. Property is considered an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home if
it is a facility for living, sleeping, and sanitation and may include facilities
for eating and cooking for occupancy other than a family which does not
qualify as a group home for the disabled.
b. In order for an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home to be an
allowed use, a Special Use Permit must be obtained. The application must
include the following:
i. A site plan.
ii. A copy of the recorded deed or sales contract.
iii. A project narrative, including:
1) Benefits of the use to the area.
2) An explanation of the proposed use.
3) A schedule for development.
4) Compatibility of the proposed use with adjacent uses and
character of the area.
5) Details of the operation (number of residents to be served,
number of individuals employed on-site, rules and regulations for
operation and for residents, resident/facility schedules, security
procedures, etc.).
iv. A notarized statement detailing qualifications.
v. Copies of applicable licenses.
vi. Any additional information or plans, if deemed necessary.

<!-- page 173 -->
Section 1917 – Guest Houses/Accessory Dwelling Units
1. Performance Standards. Approval of Guest Houses shall require compliance with
the following performance standards:
a. A maximum of: one Guest House is permitted per lot.
i. One attached and one detached guest house are permitted on any lot
under one (1) acre in size.
ii. One attached and two detached guest houses are permitted on any lot
that is one (1) acre or more in size.
b. Guest Houses shall only be permitted in those all zoning districts in which
they are listed under Use Regulations as an allowed use  single-family
dwellings are an allowed use. The following are the only permissible zoning
districts for Guest Houses addressed under this section:
i. A-General
ii. RU-20
iii. RU-10
iv. RU-5
v. RU-1
c. Guest Houses shall only be permitted on lots which have a minimum lot size
of ten thousand (10,000) square feet or more.
d. Guest Houses are limited to seventy (70) percent of the livable square
footage of the primary dwelling.
e. Guest Houses shall be sited no fewer than five feet (5’) from any rear or side
property line.  Front yard setbacks shall be those required of a primary
dwelling in the zoning district in which the house is located  meet all
setbacks required of a primary dwelling in the zoning district in which they
are located.
f. Single family Dwellings, including conventional site-built homes,
Manufactured Homes (including rehabilitated Mobile Homes) and Factory
Built Buildings may be used as a Guest House in zones where those
structures are allowed as primary Dwelling Units. Travel Trailers and
Recreational Vehicles are prohibited as Guest Houses.

<!-- page 174 -->
g. Park models may be used as a Guest House in zones where guest houses are
permissible, provided they are placed on a permanent or semi-permanent
foundation. All other permitting requirements shall apply to park models.
h. All utilities may be on separate meters than the primary dwelling, unless
otherwise prohibited by a utility company.
i. Whenever possible, Guest Houses shall not be located in front of the
primary dwelling.
2. Applications. An application for a Guest House shall be submitted to the
Department on a form approved by the Director.  The application shall be
accompanied by:
a. An accurate site plan and description of the property.
b. Preliminary floor plans and elevations of all proposed buildings.
c. An estimate of the valuation of the proposed construction.
d. Satisfactory evidence of the applicant’s ability and intention to proceed with
construction in accordance with the plans within six (6) months after the
Guest House Permit is granted.
3. Permits and Administration.
a. No guest house permit is required for attached guest houses.
b. Applicable Building, Environmental Quality, and Engineering Permits
apply to all guest houses. Department review shall be conducted through the
issuance of such permits.
ARTICLE 23 – DEFINITIONS
41. Dwelling Unit, Accessory: See definition for Guest House.
42.Guest House: A self-contained living unit that is on the same lot or parcel as a
single-family dwelling of greater square footage than the accessory dwelling
unit, that includes its own sleeping and sanitation facilities and that may
include its own kitchen facilities.

<!-- page 175 -->
43.Manufactured Home Park: Any parcel of land upon which three (3) or more
Manufactured Homes or Recreational Vehicles are located occupied for
dwelling or sleeping purposes, regardless of whether or not a charge is made for
such accommodations. This definition does not apply to guest houses permitted
under Section 1917 of the Zoning Ordinance.
115. Recreational Vehicle Park: Any parcel of land upon which two (2) or more
Recreational Vehicles are located for transient dwelling purposes.  This
definition does not apply to park models placed as guest houses permitted
under Section 1917 of the Zoning Ordinance.
151. Wireless Communication Facility, Non-Residential: A property contained
within the RU-5, RU-10, RU-10, RU-20, IND-1, or IND-2 zoning districts.
Additionally, a property contained within the RU-1, A-General, or Commercial-
Residential zoning districts when not within a platted subdivision.
152. Wireless Communication Facility, Residential: A property contained within
the R1-43, R1-10, R-2, or R-3 zoning districts.  Additionally, a property
contained within the RU-1, A-General, or Commercial-Residential zoning
districts when within a platted subdivision.

The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Multi-Family Zoning

RESOLUTION ____-25
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS ADOPTING
AN AMENDMENT TO THE NAVAJO COUNTY ZONING ORDINANCE
WHEREAS, Planning & Development Services Staff has recommended the amendment
of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, Ordinance No. Z90-1; and
WHEREAS, a duly noticed public hearing on said amendments was held by the Navajo
County Planning and Zoning Commission on August 21st, 2025; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Zoning Ordinance Amendment is consistent with the public health, safety, and general welfare
and should be approved as set forth herein; and
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby adopt
the proposed changes and amendments to Sections 502, 602, 702, 1916, and 1917 and Article 23
of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance as follows:
SECTION 1. That Sections 502, 602, 702, 1916, and 1917 and Article 23 are hereby amended as
follows (new text is shown in underline, and text proposed to be deleted is shown as strikeout):

1. Guest houses.

1. Guest houses.

The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Commercial-Residential Zoning
District:

i. One attached and one detached guest house are permitted on any lot under one

ii. One attached and two detached guest houses are permitted on any lot that is
one (1) acre or more in size.
b. Guest Houses shall only be permitted in those all zoning districts in which they are
listed under Use Regulations as an allowed use  single-family dwellings are an
allowed use . The following are the only permissible zoning districts for Guest
Houses addressed under this section:
i. A-General
ii. RU-20
iii. RU-10
iv. RU-5
v. RU-1
c. Guest Houses shall only be permitted on lots which have a minimum lot size of ten
thousand (10,000) square feet or more.
d. Guest Houses are limited to seventy (70) percent of the livable square footage of
the primary dwelling.
e. Guest Houses shall be sited no fewer than five feet (5’) from any rear or side
property line.  Front yard setbacks shall be those required of a primary dwelling in
the zoning district in which the house is located  meet all setbacks required of a
primary dwelling in the zoning district in which they are located.
f. Single family Dwellings, including conventional site-built homes, Manufactured
Homes (including rehabilitated Mobile Homes) and Factory Built Buildings may
be used as a Guest House in zones where those structures are allowed as primary
Dwelling Units. Travel Trailers and Recreational Vehicles are prohibited as Guest
Houses.

<!-- page 189 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
g. Park models may be used as a Guest House in zones where guest houses are
permissible, provided they are placed on a permanent or semi-permanent
foundation. All other permitting requirements shall apply to park models.
h. All utilities may be on separate meters than the primary dwelling, unless otherwise
prohibited by a utility company.
i. Whenever possible, Guest Houses shall not be located in front of the primary
dwelling.
2. Applications. An application for a Guest House shall be submitted to the Department on a
form approved by the Director.  The application shall be accompanied by:
a. An accurate site plan and description of the property.
b. Preliminary floor plans and elevations of all proposed buildings.
c. An estimate of the valuation of the proposed construction.
d. Satisfactory evidence of the applicant’s ability and intention to proceed with
construction in accordance with the plans within six (6) months after the Guest
House Permit is granted.
3. Permits and Administration.
a. No guest house permit is required for attached guest houses.
b. Applicable Building, Environmental Quality, and Engineering Permits apply to all
guest houses. Department review shall be conducted through the issuance of such
permits.
ARTICLE 23 – DEFINITIONS
41. Dwelling Unit, Accessory: See definition for Guest House.
42. Guest House: A self-contained living unit that is on the same lot or parcel as a single-
family dwelling of greater square footage than the accessory dwelling unit, that
includes its own sleeping and sanitation facilities and that may include its own kitchen
facilities.
43. Manufactured Home Park: Any parcel of land upon which three (3) or more
Manufactured Homes or Recreational Vehicles are located occupied for dwelling or

<!-- page 190 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
sleeping purposes, regardless of whether or not a charge is made for such
accommodations. This definition does not apply to guest houses permitted under
Section 1917 of the Zoning Ordinance.
115. Recreational Vehicle Park: Any parcel of land upon which two (2) or more
Recreational Vehicles are located for transient dwelling purposes.  This definition does
not apply to park models placed as guest houses permitted under Section 1917 of the
Zoning Ordinance.
151. Wireless Communication Facility, Non-Residential: A property contained within
the RU-5, RU-10, RU-10, RU-20, IND-1, or IND-2 zoning districts. Additionally, a
property contained within the RU-1, A-General, or Commercial-Residential zoning
districts when not within a platted subdivision.
152. Wireless Communication Facility, Residential: A property contained within the R1-
43, R1-10, R-2, or R-3 zoning districts.  Additionally, a property contained within the
RU-1, A-General, or Commercial-Residential zoning districts when within a platted
subdivision.
SECTION 2. Separability
Should any Article, Section, or regulation of this Ordinance be declared by the courts to be
unconstitutional or invalid, such decision shall not affect the validity of the Ordinance as a whole,
or any portion thereof other than the Article, Section, or regulation so declared to be
unconstitutional or invalid.
SECTION 3. Clerical Corrections
The County Clerk is hereby authorized to correct typographical and grammatical errors, as
well as errors of wording and punctuation, as necessary related to this ordinance as amended
herein, and to make formatting changes needed for purposes of clarity and form, or
consistency, within thirty (30) days following adoption by the Board of Supervisors.
SECTION 4. Effective Date
The amendments to the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance shall become effective on September
26th, 2025.

A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS ADOPTING
AN AMENDMENT TO THE NAVAJO COUNTY ZONING ORDINANCE
WHEREAS, Planning & Development Services Staff has recommended the amendment
of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, Ordinance No. Z90-1; and
WHEREAS, a duly noticed public hearing on said amendments was held by the Navajo
County Planning and Zoning Commission on August 21st, 2025; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Zoning Ordinance Amendment is consistent with the public health, safety, and general welfare
and should be approved as set forth herein; and
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby adopt
the proposed changes and amendments to Sections 502, 602, 702, 1916, and 1917 and Article 23
of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance as follows:
SECTION 1. That Sections 502, 602, 702, 1916, and 1917 and Article 23 are hereby amended as
follows:
Section 502 – Use Regulations
The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Single-Family Residential
Zoning Districts:
1. Guest houses.
2. Home occupations.
Section 602 – Use Regulations
The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Multi-Family Zoning Districts:
1. Guest houses.
2. Home occupations.
Section 702 – Use Regulations
The following uses require an Administrative Permit within the Commercial-Residential Zoning
District:
1. Guest houses.

<!-- page 193 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Section 1916 – Group Homes
Group Homes for the Disabled
1. Purpose: The purpose of these regulations is to permit disabled persons, as defined by state
and federal law, to reside in single-family residences in compliance with the Fair Housing
Act and Arizona state law, while maintaining the residential character of neighborhoods.
2. Applicability:
a. Zoning Confirmation: Prior to registration, requests for zoning confirmation may
be submitted to the Department to confirm the proposed location of the group home
is permitted per this Section.
b. Group Homes for the Disabled Serving Six (6) or Fewer Residents: Group Homes
for the Disabled that treat six (6) or fewer persons are not governed by this Section
and shall only be subject to the regulations of the Zoning District in which the
Group Home is established, in the same manner as any other single-family
residence, per A.R.S. § 36-582.
c. Additional Requirements of State Law: Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the State
has adopted laws or rules for the regulation of a specific type of home, such as a
Group Home for the Developmentally Disabled or an Assisted Living Home as
defined by state statutes, then any such State law or rule shall apply in addition to
the conditions listed herein and shall preempt any conflicting condition listed
herein.
3. Performance Standards: Group Homes for the Disabled shall be located, developed, and
operated in compliance with the following standards:
a. Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1,200 feet as
prescribed by A.R.S. § 36-582, as measured by the closest property lines.
b. Occupancy: The number of residents, excluding staff, shall not exceed 10.
c. Exterior Appearance: There shall be no sign or other exterior indication of a group
home visible from a street.
d. Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety Regulations:
Group homes shall be required to comply with all relevant building, environmental
health, and fire safety regulations. If a group home has one or more non-ambulatory

<!-- page 194 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
residents, building code requirements in addition to those applicable to group
homes containing no non-ambulatory residents, shall apply.
e. Licensing: Group homes shall comply with applicable licensing requirements.
f. Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on-site.
g. Reasonable Accommodations: Group homes proposing to be located within the
1,200-foot separation may be granted reasonable accommodations to locate where
proposed when it is found that the applicant has demonstrated by a preponderance
of evidence that the proposed group home will not negatively affect nearby
residents without disabilities, or the ability of nearby group homes to help their
residents integrate into the community. Nor will the proposed group home, in
combination with any existing group home, alter the residential character of the
surrounding neighborhood by creating an institutional atmosphere or de facto social
service district by clustering group homes in a neighborhood.
4. Permits and Administration:
a. Property is considered a Group Home for the Disabled under this Ordinance after
the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Department issues a Permit for a Group
Home for the Disabled for that property. Only properties serving occupants who fit
the definition of disabled under the Fair Housing Act are eligible for determination
as a Group Home for the Disabled.
b. In order to secure a permit for a group home for the disabled, an application must
be submitted on the form prescribed by the Department. The application must
include the following:
i. A site plan.
ii. A notarized statement, detailing qualifications.
iii. Copies of applicable licenses.
iv. Affidavit of compliance and other documentation indicating the property as a
group home for the disabled.
v. Any additional information, or plans, if deemed necessary.
c. If standards are not met, the home shall be considered an institutional residence or
other group home and shall be subject to the provisions of the Zoning Ordinance

<!-- page 195 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
regulating such establishments.  Status as a Group Home for the Disabled is open
to review by the Department and may be cancelled at any time, upon a
determination by the Director that the property is no longer being used for a
qualifying purpose or meeting standards laid forth in this Ordinance.
Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes
1. Purpose: The purpose of these regulations is to provide guidelines for permitting group
homes which do not qualify as group homes for the disabled.
2. Applicability:
a. Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the State has adopted laws or rules for the
regulation of a specific type of group home as defined by State statutes, then any
such State law or rule shall apply in addition to the conditions listed herein and shall
preempt any conflicting condition listed herein.
3. Performance Standards: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall be located,
developed, and operated in compliance with the following standards:
a. Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1200 feet as
measured by the closest property lines. These group homes shall also not be located
within 1200 feet from any religious, educational, or childcare facilities.
b. Zoning: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall only be allowed in
the R-2, R-3, and C-R zoning districts upon acquiring a Special Use Permit.
c. Occupancy: Occupancy shall be regulated within the zoning districts as listed
below:
i. R-2 and R-3: One resident for every bedroom within the residence.
ii. C-R: Up to two residents for every bedroom within the facility.
d. Exterior Appearance: Within R-2 and R-3 zoning districts, there shall be no sign or
exterior indication of a group home visible from the street. Within C-R zoning,
signage and exterior shall comply with the regulations of the zoning district. For
group homes within the R-2 and R-3 zoning districts, a solid wall or fence
acceptable to the Director of Planning and Development Services shall be required
around any side and rear yards.
e. Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety Regulations:
Group homes shall be required to comply with all relevant building, environmental

<!-- page 196 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
health, and fire safety regulations. If a group home has one or more non-ambulatory
residents, building code requirements in addition to those applicable to group
homes containing no non-ambulatory residents, shall apply.
f. Licensing: Group Homes shall comply with applicable licensing requirements.
g. Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on site and comply with all
parking standards. No parking will be allowed on any street or within any right-of-
way.
4. Permits and Administration:
a. Property is considered an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home if it is a
facility for living, sleeping, and sanitation and may include facilities for eating and
cooking for occupancy other than a family which does not qualify as a group home
for the disabled.
b. In order for an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home to be an allowed use,
a Special Use Permit must be obtained. The application must include the following:
i. A site plan.
ii. A copy of the recorded deed or sales contract.
iii. A project narrative, including:
1) Benefits of the use to the area.
2) An explanation of the proposed use.
3) A schedule for development.
4) Compatibility of the proposed use with adjacent uses and character of
the area.
5) Details of the operation (number of residents to be served, number of
individuals employed on-site, rules and regulations for operation and for
residents, resident/facility schedules, security procedures, etc.).
iv. A notarized statement detailing qualifications.
v. Copies of applicable licenses.

<!-- page 197 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
vi. Any additional information or plans, if deemed necessary.
Section 1917 – Guest Houses/Accessory Dwelling Units
1. Performance Standards. Approval of Guest Houses shall require compliance with the

ii. One attached and two detached guest houses are permitted on any lot that is
one (1) acre or more in size.
b. Guest Houses shall be permitted in all zoning districts in which single-family
dwellings are an allowed use.
c. Guest Houses shall be sited no fewer than five feet (5’) from any rear or side
property line.  Front yard setbacks shall be those required of a primary dwelling in
the zoning district in which the house is located.
d. Single family Dwellings, including conventional site-built homes, Manufactured
Homes (including rehabilitated Mobile Homes) and Factory Built Buildings may
be used as a Guest House in zones where those structures are allowed as primary
Dwelling Units. Travel Trailers and Recreational Vehicles are prohibited as Guest
Houses.
e. Park models may be used as a Guest House in zones where guest houses are
permissible, provided they are placed on a permanent or semi-permanent
foundation. All other permitting requirements shall apply to park models.
f. All utilities may be on separate meters than the primary dwelling, unless otherwise
prohibited by a utility company.
g. Whenever possible, Guest Houses shall not be located in front of the primary
dwelling.
2. Applications. An application for a Guest House shall be submitted to the Department on a
form approved by the Director.  The application shall be accompanied by:
a. An accurate site plan and description of the property.
b. Preliminary floor plans and elevations of all proposed buildings.

<!-- page 198 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
3. Permits and Administration.
a. No guest house permit is required for attached guest houses.
b. Applicable Building, Environmental Quality, and Engineering Permits apply to all
guest houses. Department review shall be conducted through the issuance of such
permits.
ARTICLE 23 – DEFINITIONS
41. Dwelling Unit, Accessory: See definition for Guest House.
42. Guest House: A self-contained living unit that is on the same lot or parcel as a single-
family dwelling of greater square footage than the accessory dwelling unit, that
includes its own sleeping and sanitation facilities and that may include its own kitchen
facilities.
43. Manufactured Home Park: Any parcel of land upon which three (3) or more

115. Recreational Vehicle Park: Any parcel of land upon which two (2) or more

151. Wireless Communication Facility, Non-Residential: A property contained within

property contained within the RU-1, A-General, or Commercial-Residential zoning
districts when not within a platted subdivision.
152. Wireless Communication Facility, Residential: A property contained within the R1-
43, R1-10, R-2, or R-3 zoning districts.  Additionally, a property contained within the

Should any Article, Section, or regulation of this Ordinance be declared by the courts to be

TXT-A 25-002 NAVAJO COUNTY ASSISTED LIVING
HOMES ADMINISTRATIVE PROCEDURES, GUEST
HOUSES/ACCESSORY DWELLING UNITS, AND
DEFINITIONS UPDATE
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS AGENDA ITEM

<!-- page 203 -->
ASSISTED LIVING HOME
ADMINISTRATIVE PROCEDURES

<!-- page 204 -->
BACKGROUND/SUMMARY – ASSISTED LIVING HOME
ADMINISTRATIVE PROCEDURES
• A.R.S. § 11-820.02 is already signed into law
• This law requires the following:
• If a county has zoning regulations that restrict the distance between assisted living homes, the county shall
establish an administrative procedure in which the deviation from the separation requirement may be granted as a
reasonable accommodation under the fair housing act
• For the purposes of this law, “assisted living home” is defined as, “a residential care institution, including an adult
foster care home, that provides or contracts to provide supervisory care services, personal care services or directed care
services on a continuous basis and provides resident rooms to ten or fewer residents.”
4TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 205 -->
SUMMARY OF CHANGES
• Section 1916 (Group Homes)
• Add a subparagraph named “Reasonable Accommodations” under "Performance Standards” for Group Homes for
the Disabled, which allows applicants to administratively have a deviation from Navajo County’s twelve-hundred
foot (1200’) separation required between these types of group homes.
5TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 206 -->
GUEST HOUSES/ACCESSORY
DWELLING UNITS

<!-- page 207 -->
BACKGROUND – GUEST HOUSES/ACCESSORY
DWELLING UNITS
• HB 2928 was introduced, passed, and signed by the Governor into law during 2025 Arizona Legislative Session
• This new law will create A.R.S. § 11-810.01
• These new provisions affect regulation of Accessory Dwelling Units (ADUs) in counties
• New legislation will take effect September 26th, 2025
7TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 208 -->
SUMMARY OF LEGISLATION – GUEST
HOUSES/ACCESSORY DWELLING UNITS
• Counties shall adopt regulations that allow on any lot or parcel where single-family dwellings are allowed:
• At least one attached and one detached ADU as a permitted use
• A minimum of one additional detached ADU as a permitted use on a lot or parcel that is one acre or more in size
• Counties shall adopt regulations as required by A.R.S. § 11-810.01 by January 1st, 2026
• If a county fails to adopt these regulations within this timeframe, ADUs shall be allowed on all lots or parcels zoned
for residential use in the county, without limits
8TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 209 -->
SUMMARY OF LEGISLATION – GUEST
HOUSES/ACCESSORY DWELLING UNITS
• Counties may not:
• Prohibit use of single-family dwellings or ADUs as long-term rental housing
• Require familial, marital, employment, or other preexisting relationship between owner/occupant of a single-family dwelling and
occupant of an ADU on the same lot
• Require that lot have additional parking to accommodate an ADU or require fees in lieu of this parking
• Require than an ADU match the exterior design, roof pitch, or finishing materials of the single-family dwelling on the same lot
• Set restrictions for ADUs that are more restrictive than those for single-family dwellings in the same zoning area
• Set rear or side setbacks for ADU units that are more than five feet from the property line
• Require improvements of public streets as a conditions of allowing an ADU, except to repair a street disturbed as a result of the
construction of the ADU
• Require a restrictive covenant concerning an ADU on a lot or parcel zoned for residential use by a single-family dwelling
9TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 210 -->
SUMMARY OF CHANGES
• Sections 502 (Single-Family Zoning Districts), 602 (Multi-Family Zoning Districts), 702 (Commercial-Residential
Zoning District):
• Add guest house as an allowed use that requires an Administrative Permit
10TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 211 -->
SUMMARY OF CHANGES
• Section 1917 (Guest Houses/Accessory Dwelling Units):
• Add provisions for guest houses to be permitted according to law on lots under one acre, as well as lots one acre or
more in size
• Remove allowed zoning districts for guest houses that conflict with required provisions of the new law
• Remove minimum lot size requirements that conflict with required provisions of the new law
• Remove size limitation requirements from guest house provisions, ensuring no conflict with new law
• Add provisions for guest houses to be allowed within five feet of rear and side lot lines
• Remove additionally required information that does not prove useful in reviewing guest house permit applications
• Clarify separate guest house permits are not required for attached guest houses
11TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 212 -->
SUMMARY OF CHANGES
• Article 23 (Definitions):
• Better define “Guest House” to be the same as introduced into State law
• Introduce “Accessory Dwelling Unit” to be defined the same as “Guest House,” to prevent any confusion with the
new State law regarding ADUs
• Amend the definitions of “Manufactured Home Park” and “Recreational Vehicle Park” to not include guest houses,
as this would trigger additional permitting that would not be in compliance with new State law
12TXT-A 25-002

<!-- page 213 -->
RECOMMENDATION
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff
recommend approval of the update to the Navajo County
Zoning Ordinance.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a.        
Meeting Date: 08/26/2025  
Title: Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Teesto Chapter
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Nicole Reske, Tribal Government Relations Liaison
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Nicole Reske
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of an Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Teesto Chapter for
assistance with routine maintenance of heavy equipment. 
Background:
Teesto Chapter is responsible for maintaining approximately 57 miles of roadway within the Teesto
Chapter service area supporting school bus routes, emergency medical access and daily transportation for
residents of Teesto, Dilkon, Indian Wells and Whitecone Chapters. To ensure continued service, funding
is needed for critical supplies including blades, filters, oil, hydraulic fluid, and routine maintenance
components. With the support from Navajo County, Teesto Chapter will be able to sustain their road
grading efforts and continue collaborative service alongside BIA and NDOT - ultimately benefiting the
health, safety, and mobility of our community members.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.

Fiscal Year: FY 2026 
Budgeted Y/N: Y 
Amount Requested: 4000

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT
between  the
COUNTY OF NAVAJO and TEESTO CHAPTER
concerning the MAINTENANCE OF BIA INVENTORY ROUTES
THIS AGREEMENT is entered into as of August 26th, 2025 (the 'Effective Date") by and
between the NAVAJO COUNTY, a politician subdivision of the State of Arizona (the "County"),
and the TEESTO CHAPTER, a political subdivision of the Navajo Nation located within Navajo
County and the Navajo Reservation (the "Chapter").
WHEREAS, the parties are authorized by A.R .S. §§ 11-951 and 11-952 to enter
intergovernmental agreements for cooperative action; and
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 11-251(4) authorizes the County to maintain all public roads within its
boundaries; and
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 11-251(29) authorizes the Board of Supervisor of the County to enter into
agreements for  the Indian lands, with the  government of the United States acting  through
its duly authorized officers or agents pursuant to any act of Congress; and
WHEREAS,  the above statues  have been interpreted by  the Arizona Attorney  General  in
Opinion No.  186-029 to authorize the expenditure of funds  for the maintenance of all  public
roads within  Indian Country  (as defined in  18 U.S.C. § 1151);
WHEREAS, the Code of Federal Regulations provides in 25 C.F.R. § 170.6 that "The
administration and maintenance of Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges is basically a function
of the local government. Subject to the availability of funds, the Commissioner (of Indian Affairs)
shall maintain, or case to be maintained, those approved roads on the Federal-Aid Indian Road
System;" and
WHEREAS, 25 C.F.R. § 170.7 provides that "The Commissioner may enter into an agreement
with the State (which is defined to include political subdivisions such as maintenance of certain
Indian Reservation Road and Bridges, especially at those locations where road projects serve
non-Indian land as well as Indian land;" and
WHEREAS,  the County  has received  from the Chapter  a request  for HURF  funds to be
used in heavy equipment parts  for Chapter equipment which  will result in the maintenance
of BIA and NNDOT  Public Routes  serving  the Chapter Community,
NOW, WHEREAS, pursuant  to their  authority  as set forth above,  the parties  hereby  agree
as follows:

<!-- page 217 -->
Navajo  County- For  Official  Use Only
1. Allocation  of Highway  User Revenue  Funds . The Navajo  County  Board of
Supervisors, by motion duly passed at its regular meeting  on August 26th, 2025
approved the expenditure of up  to $4,000 in HURF funds  (the "Allocated
Funds") to  be used for the maintenance of Teesto  Chapter Heavy Equipment
(list shown in Exhibit 1) which maintains BIA Public Routes which  are located on
the Navajo Reservation in  Navajo County, serving the school District and
community.
2. Purposes.  The Chapter agrees  that the Allocated Funds  shall be used solely  for
the maintenance of  Teesto  Chapter  Equipment which  maintains BIA  public routes
which are located on  the Navajo Reservation in Navajo County, serve  the Chapter
and meet  the HURF criteria. This may include the employment of personnel of
contractors; the acquisition, operation, maintenance and repair of equipment; and
the purchase of necessary materials and parts.
3. Disbursement  of Allocated  Funds . The allocated  Funds  shall be utilized  to
purchase material or parts by the County upon request by Teesto  Chapter .
4. Chapter Permission to Maintain Routes . It is understood that the  Chapter has
an agreement set in place with Bureau of  Indian Affairs (BIA) and  Navajo Nation
Division  of Transportation  (NDOT) to  maintain roads  within Chapter service area.
5. County Not Responsible for Maintenance.  The Chapter shall be  responsible
for identifying, prioritizing, scheduling and performing all maintenance activities
for which the Allocated Funds are used in whole or part. It is understood that the
County's sole obligation hereunder is  to make payment of the Allocated Funds.
6. Term and  Termination.  This Agreement shall expire June 30, 2026 of its own
accord when  the Allocated Funds  have been disbursed  in their  entirely.  This
Agreement may  be terminated by any  parties.  This Agreement is  subject to
cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. 55 38-511.

Motion before the Board:

gravel material for N8065. 
Background:
The Navajo Division of Transportation has requested assistance with the procurement of gravel material
for N8065 within Black Mesa Chapter, in Navajo County. During inclement weather, the road becomes
muddy and unpassable. The placement of 8 miles of gravel will help in stabilizing the roadway surface.
This route is primarily used by school buses, work commuters, first responders and residents.
Additionally, this route is part of the NDOT regional road maintenance program. Labor and maintenance
work will be performed by NDOT.

CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES 
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of August 26, 2025, between Navajo County Flood Control 
District ("District") and JE Fuller Hydrology & Geomorphology, Inc (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, District requires certain professional services for Navajo County 
ALERT System Maintenance and Improvements for 2026 (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Work dated 
February 11, 2025, and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s schedule 
and fee proposal dated February 11, 2025, in a competent and professional manner to 
the satisfaction of District.  The Scope of Work and the Consultant’s schedule and fee 
proposal are attached hereto and by this reference incorporated herein.  If any 
incorporated term is inconsistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control. 
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perform this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positions.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without District’s prior 
written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agent of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be withheld from any payment by District, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters. 
 
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without District’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed

on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15, 18, 21  and 22.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of car e and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accordance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by District shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
District or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary , as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the

6. Payment.  District shall pay to Consultant a not-to- exceed sum of sixty eight 
thousand dollars ($68,000) as payment in full for all services rendered by Consultant 
pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 30 days of District’s 
receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall detail the work 
performed during the billing period. 
 
7. Defects in Work.  District may reject any work product that fails to meet customary

resolved.

Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming District as an

RE: Navajo County ALERT System Maintenance and Improvements for FY 2026 
 
Dear Donald,  
 
Thank you for the opportunity to provide annual services in support of the Navajo County 
ALERT/Flood Warning System per the following scope of work.  This scope outlines all the 
tasks JE Fuller is currently aware of, but other tasks can be included if Navajo County 
requests other tasks.   
 
Scope of Work 
 
This scope includes routine preventative maintenance for the Navajo County ALERT 
System, ALERT supplies, base station server upgrades and data acquisition software 
deployment as follows. 
 
ALERT Maintenance- $60,000 
 
Scheduled maintenance and calibration shall be performed at the following 30 sites: 
 
Base Station  
Black Canyon Wash (1878)-S LCR in Joseph City-Obed Bridge (1743)-P&S 
Buckskin Wash (1808)-P&S LCR Winslow (1729)- P 
Bunger Point (1705)-P Morgan Wash (1849)- P&S 
Chevelon Butte (1800)-P Oklahoma Flat (1721)-P 
Cottonwood Wash in Taylor (1829)-P&S Phoenix Park Wash (1893)-P&S 
Dreamy Draw (1725)-P&S Pinedale Ridge (1778)-P 
Dutch Joe (1843)-P Pinetop Country Club (1712)-P 
Heber Repeater (1871)-P Porter Mountain Repeater (1804)-Repeater 
Heber Snotel (1885)-P Schoens Dam (1815)-S 
Holbrook Base (1856)-P Show Low Lake (1892)-P 
Joe City at Route 66 (1771)-P&S Silver Creek in Snowflake (1785)-P&S 
LCR @ Holbrook (1764)-P Stermer Ridge (1722)-P 
White Mtn Clay Targets (1704)-P Millet Swale Dam (1742)-P&S 
Show Low Well #5 (1708)-P Show Low Creek @ US 60 (1711)-P&S 
LCR Hunt (1701)-P White Mountain Lake (1822)-P&S 
*P is a station with precipitation measurements and S is a station with stage measurements.

<!-- page 246 -->
P a g e | 2 
 
  www. jefuller.com | www.jefullerdata.com 
Included herein is an outline of tasks for maintenance at precipitation stations, water level 
stations, repeaters, and the base station. 
 
Precipitation Stations 
 
Routine maintenance at precipitation stations shall include the following elements: 
 
• Inspect, clean and calibrate the tipping bucket rain gauge and funnel assembly. A static 
calibration using 72.9 ml of water per tip is sufficient to track calibration changes. 
 
• Send 10 tipping bucket tips sequentially by hand to observe for skips, 
 
• Inspect the antenna, coaxial cables, seals and attendant lightning protection system, 
 
• Download logged data and store on a field laptop computer, 
 
• Remove and replace the main system battery, 
 
• Remove and replace the data logger battery (if applicable), 
 
• Inspect the transmitter for signs of moisture or damage, 
 
• Perform power out and reflection testing on the transmitter. Reflected power should not  
 exceed 10% of the output power. 
 
• Check and test the solar panel at the regulator. Input to the regulator should be between 15 
volts DC and 20 volts DC and output should be between 13 volts DC and 14 volts DC. 
 
• Remove and replace transmitter desiccant before re-assembling the station. 
 
• Level tipping bucket and replace top section funnel assembly.  
Water Level Stations  
Water level stations require the same maintenance and calibration steps as a precipitation-only 
station plus additional cleaning, checking and calibration of the water level sensor. Navajo 
County employs two types of level sensors; pressure transducers and radar sensors.  
Pressure transducers should be checked and calibrated at least once per year and after 
significant flow events. The pressure transducer calibration shall be checked throughout its full 
range using a precision air pressure tester such as those made by Druck and Fluke.  
Radar sensors require very little maintenance and calibration since they are non-contact type 
sensors. During each maintenance visit, the transducer face will be gently wiped clean using a 
clean, damp cloth. Also, the distance from the transducer to the target (either the sand bed or the 
water surface) will be verified by direct measurement.  Additionally, the sensor will be verified at 
a second distance to ensure span is accurate.

<!-- page 247 -->
P a g e | 3 
 
  www. jefuller.com | www.jefullerdata.com 
Repeater Maintenance 
Maintenance at repeater stations shall include those tasks listed above for precipitation stations 
except for maintenance to the tipping bucket precipitation sensor if not installed at site. 
Base Station Maintenance 
Base station maintenance has been ongoing by JE Fuller via the VPN connection established by 
the county for remote access to the server. Maintenance included for the FY 2026 year shall 
include routine monthly checks via VPN connection to verify that data are being received.  Onsite 
maintenance shall include the following elements: 
 
• Visual inspection of the receiver infrastructure, including antenna, coaxial cable, grounding, 
     connectors and weather seals. 
 
• Inspect and verify proper operation of the receiver and decoder. 
 
• Test for coaxial cable signal attenuation with output/reflection test. 
 
• Verify that all internal cables are securely fastened. 
 
• Work with IT to verify UPS backup power and redundancy possibilities. 
 
 
Base Station Server Software Maintenance-$8,000 
 
The annual maintenance fee of the software and website is approximately $8,000. 
JE Fuller can coordinate and provide rainfall data and notification modifications as requested by 
Navajo County.

<!-- page 248 -->
P a g e | 4 
 
  www. jefuller.com | www.jefullerdata.com 
Fee 
JE Fuller will charge monthly as work is completed.  Based on the items provided in this scope, 
JE Fuller proposes a total NTE amount of $68,000 for FY 2026.

the contract for one additional year (September 14, 2025, to September 13, 2026) with no price increase
Background:
On August 21, 2006, the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District was formed by Resolution #68-06.

maintenance and related improvements. This contract may be renewed up to four additional one-year
terms with an increase up to 5% year to year. Both the district and contractor are in concurrence;
therefore, we are recommending that the contact be extended one additional year (September 14, 2025 to
September 13, 2026) with no increase.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a.        
Meeting Date: 08/26/2025  
Title: Multistate Opioid Settlement Agreement and Revised Bankruptcy Plan with Purdue
Pharmacueticals and Sackler Family
Submitted For: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney 
Submitted By: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Jason Moore
Motion before the Board:
EXECUTIVE SESSION: pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A)(3) for the Board to receive legal advice
from its attorney regarding the multi-state opioid litigation pending in federal court against multiple
manufacturers, distributors, and retailers of prescription opioid medications.
Background:
Navajo County is a party to the multi-state opioid litigation pending in federal court against multiple
manufacturers, distributors, and retailers of prescription opioid medications. The Board has already
approved several settlements against various parties and is already receiving settlement proceeds which
are being used to remediate the opioid crisis.
In 2021, Navajo County approved a settlement plan with Purdue and the Sackler Family as well as a
bankruptcy reorganization plan for Purdue Pharma.  Ultimately, however, the bankruptcy plan was
rejected by the United States Supreme Court.   As a result, the Plaintiffs in the case and Purdue/Sackler
have been negotiating a new settlement proposal and bankruptcy plan.  The Board is being asked to
approve both a revised direct settlement with Purdue and the Sackler Family today along with its approval
of a revised bankruptcy plan of reorganization for Purdue Pharma.
In addition, proposed settlements have been reached with eight manufacturers of generic prescription
opioid medications.  Known as the "G8" settlement, approval will result in a final settlement with those
manufacturers.
Detailed information about the direct settlement with the Purdue and the Sacklers, the revised Purdue
bankruptcy reorganization plan (2025), and the G8 settlement are provided in the confidential
memorandum and analysis of Navajo County's outside legal counsel, Keller Rohrback.
Recommendation: 
It is recommended that the Board of Supervisors approve the Pudue/Sackler and G8 settlements, the
Purdue reorganization plan,  and authorize counsel to execute the appropriate Participation Packages on
behalf of Navajo County and to vote "yes" on the Purdue bankruptcy reorganization plan (2025).

TO LOCAL POLITICAL SUBDIVISIONS:
 
THIS PACKAGE CONTAINS DOCUMENTATION TO PARTICIPATE IN THE NEW 
NATIONAL OPIOIDS SETTLEMENT. YOU MUST TAKE ACTION IN ORDER TO 
PARTICIPATE.
Deadline:   September 30, 2025  
A new proposed national opioids settlement has been reached with Purdue (and  
certain of its affiliates) and the Sackler family. This Participation Package is a follow-
up communication to the Notice of New National Opioids Settlement recently received 
electronically by your subdivision. 
The  proposed  settlement  is  being  implemented  in  connection  with  Purdue’s 
bankruptcy proceedings, and consists of, among other things, a settlement of  
Purdue’s claims against the Sacklers and certain other parties (referred to as the  
“Purdue Estate Settlement”), and settlements of direct claims against the Sacklers  
held by States, local governments and other creditors (collectively, the “Purdue Direct 
Settlement”, and together with the Estate Settlement, the “Purdue Settlement”).  The 
Purdue Direct Settlement for States and local governments is documented in the  
Governmental Entity and Shareholder Direct Settlement Agreement.
You are receiving this Participation Package because all eligible States and territories, 
including Arizona, are participating in the Purdue Direct Settlement.
This electronic envelope contains: 
 The Participation Form for the Purdue Direct Settlement, including a release of 
any claims
The Participation Form must be executed, without alteration, and submitted 
on  or  before  September  30,  2025,  in  order  for  your  subdivision  to  be 
considered for initial participation calculations and payment eligibility under 
the Purdue Direct Settlement. 
Based upon subdivision participation forms received on or before  September 30,  
2025,  the  subdivision  participation  rate  will  be  used  to  determine  whether 
participation is sufficient for the Purdue Settlement to move forward and whether a  
state earns its maximum potential payment under the Purdue Direct Settlement. If  
the Purdue Settlement moves forward and goes effective, your release will become  
Docusign Envelope ID: 86F48E0A-C336-42E6-9966-EFF6301AF723

<!-- page 259 -->
effective. If the Purdue Settlement does not move forward, that release will not  
become effective. 
Any subdivision that does  not participate in the Purdue Direct Settlement cannot  
directly share in the Purdue Direct Settlement funds, even if other subdivisions in the 
state are participating and sharing in those Purdue Direct Settlement funds. Any  
subdivision that does  not participate may also reduce the amount of money for  
programs to remediate the opioid crisis in its state.  In all past national opioids  
settlements, Arizona has been able to get 100% participation of all 15 counties and 91 
cities and towns.  Our goal is to again reach 100% participation. The One Arizona  
Distribution of Opioids Settlement Funds Agreement (“One Arizona Agreement”) will 
apply to the Purdue Direct Settlement meaning that the same restrictions on and  
reporting of use of funds for Approved Purposes and the same allocation of funds to all 
106 local governments will apply.  The text of the One Arizona Agreement is located 
at  https://nationalopioidsettlement.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/Final-One-
AZ-Distribution-Agreement-Signed.pdf. 
You are encouraged to discuss the terms and benefits of the Purdue Settlement with 
your counsel, your Attorney General’s Office, and other contacts within your state. 
Information and documents regarding the Purdue Settlement, including a complete  
copy of the Governmental Entity and Shareholder Direct Settlement Agreement, can 
be  found  on  the  national  settlement  website  at  
https://nationalopioidsettlement.com/purdue-sacklers-settlements/. This  website  
will be supplemented as additional documents are created.
How to return signed forms:
There are three methods for returning the executed  Participation Form  and any  
supporting documentation to the Implementation Administrator:
(1)Electronic  Signature  via  DocuSign :  Executing  the  Participation  Form  
electronically  through  DocuSign  will  return  the  signed  form  to  the  
Implementation Administrator and associate your form with your subdivision’s 
records. Electronic signature is the most efficient method for returning 
the   Participation Form  , allowing for more timely participation and the   
potential  to  meet  higher  settlement  payment  thresholds,  and  is  
therefore strongly encouraged. 
(2)Manual  Signature  returned  via  DocuSign :  DocuSign  allows  forms  to  be 
downloaded,  signed  manually,  then  uploaded  to  DocuSign  and  returned 
automatically to the Implementation Administrator. Please be sure to complete 
all  fields.  As  with  electronic  signature,  returning  a  manually  signed 
Participation Form via DocuSign will associate your signed forms with your  
subdivision’s records.
(3)Manual Signature returned via electronic mail: If your subdivision is unable to 
return an executed Participation Form using DocuSign, the signed Participation

GOVERNMENTAL ENTITY & SHAREHOLDER  
SETTLEMENT AGREEMENT 
This Settlement Agreement, dated as of [●], 2025 (the “Agreement”), sets forth the terms of 
settlement between and among the Settling States, the MDT, solely as of the Effective Date, with respect 
to Article 4 , or otherwise as expressly set forth herein , the Participating Subdivisions , and the Payment 
Parties (as those terms are defined below). Upon the Effective Date  and satisfaction of the conditions set 
forth in Article 2  and Article 8 , this Agreement will be binding on the Settling States, Participating 
Subdivisions and the Payment Parties. This Agreement will then be filed as part of a Consent Judgment in 
the respective courts of each of the Settling States, pursuant to the terms set forth in Article 8. 
This Agreement addresses and resolves the Settli ng State and Participating Subdivision  alleged 
direct claims against the Shareholder Released Parties and claims against the Released Parties. However, it 
is only one part of a set of agreements addressed through the Plan. Party obligations and agreement t erms 
are also addressed in the Plan and its related documents, including the Master Settlement Agreement  and 
the Governmental Remediation Trust Agreement ; provided, that (i) in the event of any conflict between 
this Agreement and the Governmental Remediation Trust Agreement, the terms of this Agreement shall 
govern; (ii) in the event of any conflict between this Agreement and the Master Settlement Agreement,  
relating to payment determinations pursuant to this Agreement, enforcement, and/or release terms set forth 
herein, the  terms  of  this  Agreement shall govern, (iii) in the event of any other conflict between this 
Agreement and the Master  Settlement Agreement, the terms of the Master Settlement Agreement shall 
govern, and (iv) in the event of an y conflict between this Agreement and the Plan or Confirmation Order 
(a) with respect to any matter affecting, or a dispute involving, a party that is not a Party or otherwise bound 
by this Agreement, the Plan or Confirmation Order shall govern, as applica ble, and (b) with respect to all 
other matters, this Agreement shall govern .  This Agreement shall not be enforced against any Person not 
party to or otherwise bound by  this Agreement.  The determination of whether there is any inconsistency 
between this Agreement and the Plan or Confirmation Order shall be made by the Bankruptcy Court.   
ARTICLE 1. 
DEFINITIONS; INTERPRETATION PROVISIONS 
Section 1.01 Unless otherwise specified, the following definitions apply: 
“A-Side Payment Party” has the meaning set forth in the Master Settlement Agreement.  
“Accelerated Payment States” means certain States that qualify for an accelerated payment 
schedule with Annual Remediation Payments being made over nine or ten Payment Dates. If they become 
Settling States, Connecticut and Oregon are scheduled to receive payments through Payment Date 9; and 
Kentucky, Maine, Nevada, New Hampshire, New Mexico, Utah, West Virginia  and the Oklahoma 
Subdivisions (to the extent they are Participating Subdivisions) are scheduled to receive payments through 
Payment Date 10. 
“Advisory Committee” has the meaning set forth in Section 5.05(b)(iv). 
“Agreement” means this agreement, as set forth above. For the avoidance of doubt, this Agreement 
is inclusive of all exhibits. 
“Alleged Harms” means the alleged past, present, and future financial, societal, public nuisance, 
and other harms, as well as any expenditures related to the foregoing, arising out of the conduct, omission, 
or liability of the Debtors and relating to Covered Conduct, including the alleged use, misuse, and abuse of 
Products, non-exclusive examples of which are described in the documents listed on Exhibit A, that have 
19-23649-shl    Doc 7592    Filed 06/17/25    Entered 06/17/25 02:03:03    Main Document 
Pg 11 of 82

<!-- page 276 -->
2 
allegedly arisen as a re sult of the physical and bodily injuries sustained by individuals suffering from 
opioid-related addiction, abuse, death, and other related diseases and disorders, and that have allegedly been 
caused by Shareholder Released Parties. 
“Allocation Statute” means a state law that governs allocation, distribution, and/or use of some or 
all of the Settlement Fund amounts allocated to that Settling State and/or its Subdivisions. An Allocation 
Statute may, without limitation, contain a Statutory Trust, further restr ict expenditures of funds, form an 
advisory committee, establish oversight and reporting requirements, or address other default provisions and 
other matters related to the funds. An Allocation Statute is not required to address all three (3) types of 
funds comprising the Settlement Fund or all default provisions. 
“Annual Fees Payment” means the amounts payable by the Payment Groups on each Payment Date 
comprised of the State Direct Expenses payment and the Local Government Costs and Expenses Fund  
payment as determined pursuant to this Agreement , and not including the Annual Remediation Payment.  
For the avoidance of doubt, the Annual Fees Payment does not include amounts designated pursuant to 
Section 5.9 of the Plan as attorneys’ fees and costs based on Estate Distributions. 
“Annual Remediation Payment ” means the amounts owing pursuant to the Master Settlement 
Agreement by the Payment Groups on each Payment Date as determined pursuant to this Agreement and 
comprised of the Base Payments and Incentive Paym ents. For the avoidance of doubt, the Annual 
Remediation Payment does not include amounts owning pursuant to the Master Settlement Agreement for 
Estate Distributions.  
“Appropriate Official” has the meaning set forth in Section 13.06(c). 
“Authorized Action” has the meaning set forth in Section 13.25(c). 
“B-Side Payment Party” has the meaning set forth in the Master Settlement Agreement. 
“Bankruptcy Cases” has the meaning set forth in the Master Settlement Agreement. 
“Bankruptcy Code” has the meaning set forth in the Master Settlement Agreement.  
“Bar” means either: (1) a law barring or having the effect of barring Subdivisions in a Settling State 
from maintaining Shareholder Released Claims against Shareholder Released Parties  (either through a 
direct bar or through a grant of authority to release claim s and the exercise of such authority in full) or (2) 
a ruling by the highest court of the Settling State (or, in a Settling State with a single intermediate court of 
appeals, the intermediate court of appeals) when setting forth the general principle that Subdivisions in the 
Settling State may not maintain any Shareholder Released Claims against Shareholder Released Parties , 
whether on the ground of this Agreement (or the release in it) or otherwise. For the avoidance of doubt, a 
law or ruling that is conditioned or predicated upon payment by a Shareholder Released Party (apart from 
the Annual Remediation Payments by any Payment Group under this Agreement) shall not constitute a Bar. 
“Base Payment” has the meaning set forth in Section 4.05. 
“Business Day” means a day, other than Saturday, Sunday or a federal holiday.  
“Case-Specific Resolution ” means e ither: (1) a law barring or having the effect of barring the 
Subdivision at issue from maintaining any Shareholder Released Claims against any Shareholder Released 
Parties (either through a direct bar or through a grant of authority to release claims and the exercise of such 
authority in full); or (2) a ruling by a court of competent jurisdiction over the Subdivision at issue that the 
19-23649-shl    Doc 7592    Filed 06/17/25    Entered 06/17/25 02:03:03    Main Document
